04Checking the basket and payment statusOne mug, two recorded entries+

Your goal

Correct a duplicate before payment and distinguish the revised basket total from a refund or completed transaction.

This lesson in the book, page 31 ↗

A Read the situation

One mug, two recorded entries

Shopper Leo has one mug priced at $8, but cashier Amira has scanned it twice. The checkout record shows two mugs and $16. No other charges apply in the exercise, and payment has not begun. Amira acknowledges the duplicate, corrects the recorded quantity through the store checkout process, and reads back one mug at $8. Leo confirms the correction. No money has been taken, no refund is required for this unstarted payment, and the exchange ends before payment completion.

Leo: Shopper / Amira: Cashier

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What is the actual quantity?

Answer and explanation

B. One mug

The shopper has one mug even though two entries were recorded.

2. What is the corrected total?

Answer and explanation

B. $8

One mug at eight dollars gives eight dollars with no additional charges.

3. What is the payment status?

Answer and explanation

A. Payment has not begun

The brief explicitly places the correction before any payment begins.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

The original record incorrectly shows two $8 mugs, totaling $16. The corrected record shows one $8 mug. This is a quantity correction before payment, not a refund of money already taken.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
checkout
Place or process for recording and paying for purchases.check the checkout total
point-of-sale system
System recording sales and payments, often abbreviated POS.use the point-of-sale system
scan
Read an item code into the checkout record.scan the item
duplicate scan
Recording the same intended item twice by mistake.correct a duplicate scan
line item
Individual product entry in a transaction record.review the line item
recorded quantity
Number of units entered in the system.correct the recorded quantity
physical quantity
Actual number of items the shopper has.verify the physical quantity
basket correction
Amendment to the selected goods before completion.make a basket correction
void entry
Cancel an incorrect recorded item under the store process.void the duplicate entry
quantity adjustment
Change to the number of units recorded.confirm the quantity adjustment
revised total
Updated amount after a correction.read back the revised total
amount due
Sum still to be paid.confirm the amount due
payment status
Stage of the payment, such as unstarted or completed.check the payment status
tender
Money or payment method offered to pay.confirm the tender amount
cash payment
Payment made using notes or coins.process a cash payment
card terminal
Device used for supported card-payment interactions.identify the card terminal
contactless payment
Payment interaction using supported contactless technology.distinguish a contactless payment
authorization
Payment-system approval stage, not equivalent to every later settlement step.check authorization status
declined payment
Payment attempt not approved through the relevant system.explain a declined payment
receipt
Record of a transaction or completed purchase details.check the receipt
refund
Return of money already paid.distinguish a refund from a correction
change
Cash returned when the tendered amount exceeds the amount due.confirm the change
customer display
Screen showing transaction information to the shopper.check the customer display
split tender
Payment for one sale using more than one payment method.explain a split-tender payment

A displayed total, a started payment, an authorization, and a completed transaction are different states. In this case payment has not begun, so do not invent a decline, reversal, or refund. Follow the actual checkout process for the correction.

C Notice the language

Make the correction visible in the explanation

A shopper should not need to infer that a total changed correctly. Acknowledge the exact error, explain the corrected quantity, and read back the revised total before payment. Avoid calling an unpaid-basket correction a refund, which can make the transaction sound further along than it is.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Acknowledge the duplicate
You are right; the mug has been recorded twice.
State the actual quantity
You have one mug, not two.
Pause before payment
Let us correct the basket before payment begins.
Explain the record
The current entry shows two at $8, totaling $16.
Name the correction
I will correct the recorded quantity through our checkout process.
Confirm the revised quantity
The record now shows one mug.
Read back the amount
The corrected total is $8.
Keep status clear
Payment has not begun.
Avoid refund language
No money has been taken, so this is not a refund.
Invite a check
Please check that the display shows one mug at $8.
Distinguish the price
The price per mug stays $8; the quantity was wrong.
Avoid a false discount
This is a correction, not a special discount.
Confirm the remaining step
Payment is still the next step.
Keep the explanation short
The duplicate is removed; one mug remains.
Avoid an invented decline
There has been no payment attempt to decline.
Close the correction
We have confirmed the corrected basket before proceeding.
6 language and precision notes
Twice versus two
Scanning the same intended item twice does not mean the shopper has two items.
Quantity versus price
The unit price is unchanged; the recorded number of mugs is corrected.
Due versus paid
Eight dollars due is not eight dollars already paid.
Correction versus refund
An unstarted payment has no paid amount to return in this scenario.
Now shows
This describes the updated record, not merely a promise to change it later.
Before proceeding
The shopper can verify the correction before the payment step begins.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which explanation is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. One mug was recorded twice; the corrected total is $8 before payment.

The correction removes an erroneous quantity while preserving the eight-dollar price.

2. Which term should not describe this correction?

Answer and explanation

C. Refund

Refund means returning paid money, but payment has not begun here.

3. What should the customer display show after correction?

Answer and explanation

D. One mug at $8

The revised basket contains one eight-dollar mug before payment begins.

4. Which statement preserves the final stage?

Answer and explanation

C. The basket is corrected; payment is still next.

No payment attempt or completion occurs during this correction exchange.

E Conversations

One mug, two recorded entries

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Leo: Shopper / Amira: Cashier

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • payment status
  • physical quantity
  • customer display
  • refund
  • Transaction completion
  • revised total
  • quantity adjustment
  • duplicate scan
  • amount due
  • line item
  1. Leo
    Hang on, could you check the screen? It says two mugs, but I've only brought one to the counter.
  2. Amira
    You are right. There is a 1. The mug has been recorded twice, and we should correct that before starting payment.
  3. Leo
    Maybe it scanned again when you moved it. The mug is here; I haven't got another one in my bag.
  4. Amira
    The 2 is one mug. The recorded quantity is two, so the issue is the number entered rather than the price of the mug.
  5. Leo
    Eight dollars each is right. It's the sixteen-dollar total that caught my eye when I looked up.
  6. Amira
    Exactly. The 3 needs correction. Two at eight dollars gives sixteen, while your one mug should total eight dollars here.
  7. Leo
    Yes, please fix it before I tap. I haven't used my card yet, so there shouldn't be a payment to undo.
  8. Amira
    The 4 is still unstarted. I will make the quantity correction through our checkout process before we move to the payment step.
  9. Leo
    Are you removing the extra mug or changing its price? I'd like the record to show what I'm actually buying.
  10. Amira
    It is a 5. The price per mug stays eight dollars; we are removing the extra recorded unit rather than applying a discount.
  11. Leo
    The quantity says one now. Could you read the total back too? The screen's a bit hard to see from this angle.
  12. Amira
    The 6 is eight dollars for one mug. There are no other charges on this purchase, and the basket now matches the item on the counter.
  13. Leo
    That's better. When you said correction, I wasn't sure whether I'd have to wait for money to come back.
  14. Amira
    This is not a 7. No money has been taken here, so we are correcting the unpaid basket rather than returning a payment.
  15. Leo
    Good, so there's no refund to wait for. I'm still at the stage of paying the eight dollars.
  16. Amira
    Correct. The 8 is eight dollars. It is still to be paid, and I will not describe the sale as paid or completed.
  17. Leo
    I can see it now: one mug, eight dollars. That matches what's on the counter.
  18. Amira
    Thank you for checking the 9. Reading back both the quantity and the amount helps confirm that the record and your purchase agree.
  19. Leo
    Thanks for sorting it out. We can move on to payment now that the item and total are right.
  20. Amira
    We have confirmed one mug at eight dollars. 10 is still ahead of us, after the payment step, so the correction has not been confused with a refund or an already finished sale.

Answers and explanations
  1. duplicate scan Turn 2. Duplicate scan means the same intended mug has been entered twice, not that the shopper selected two physical mugs.
  2. physical quantity Turn 4. Physical quantity is one mug, which the checkout record must match rather than leaving the two-entry mistake in place.
  3. line item Turn 6. Line item is the transaction entry showing the mug and its quantity; the duplicate produces the incorrect sixteen-dollar total.
  4. payment status Turn 8. Payment status is unstarted, so the cashier must not describe this as a completed charge, decline, or refund.
  5. quantity adjustment Turn 10. Quantity adjustment changes the recorded number from two mugs to one while leaving the eight-dollar item price unchanged.
  6. revised total Turn 12. Revised total is eight dollars after the duplicate quantity is corrected, with no additional charges in the supplied case.
  7. refund Turn 14. Refund would return money already paid; no money has been taken because the payment has not begun.
  8. amount due Turn 16. Amount due is the eight dollars still to be paid, distinct from money already received or returned.
  9. customer display Turn 18. Customer display lets Leo verify the corrected quantity and total before the next payment step.
  10. Transaction completion Turn 20. Transaction completion has not yet occurred; the corrected basket is ready for the separate payment and completion steps.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Leo: Hang on, could you check the screen? It says two mugs, but I've only brought one to the counter.
  2. Amira: You are right. There is a duplicate scan. The mug has been recorded twice, and we should correct that before starting payment.
  3. Leo: Maybe it scanned again when you moved it. The mug is here; I haven't got another one in my bag.
  4. Amira: The physical quantity is one mug. The recorded quantity is two, so the issue is the number entered rather than the price of the mug.
  5. Leo: Eight dollars each is right. It's the sixteen-dollar total that caught my eye when I looked up.
  6. Amira: Exactly. The line item needs correction. Two at eight dollars gives sixteen, while your one mug should total eight dollars here.
  7. Leo: Yes, please fix it before I tap. I haven't used my card yet, so there shouldn't be a payment to undo.
  8. Amira: The payment status is still unstarted. I will make the quantity correction through our checkout process before we move to the payment step.
  9. Leo: Are you removing the extra mug or changing its price? I'd like the record to show what I'm actually buying.
  10. Amira: It is a quantity adjustment. The price per mug stays eight dollars; we are removing the extra recorded unit rather than applying a discount.
  11. Leo: The quantity says one now. Could you read the total back too? The screen's a bit hard to see from this angle.
  12. Amira: The revised total is eight dollars for one mug. There are no other charges on this purchase, and the basket now matches the item on the counter.
  13. Leo: That's better. When you said correction, I wasn't sure whether I'd have to wait for money to come back.
  14. Amira: This is not a refund. No money has been taken here, so we are correcting the unpaid basket rather than returning a payment.
  15. Leo: Good, so there's no refund to wait for. I'm still at the stage of paying the eight dollars.
  16. Amira: Correct. The amount due is eight dollars. It is still to be paid, and I will not describe the sale as paid or completed.
  17. Leo: I can see it now: one mug, eight dollars. That matches what's on the counter.
  18. Amira: Thank you for checking the customer display. Reading back both the quantity and the amount helps confirm that the record and your purchase agree.
  19. Leo: Thanks for sorting it out. We can move on to payment now that the item and total are right.
  20. Amira: We have confirmed one mug at eight dollars. Transaction completion is still ahead of us, after the payment step, so the correction has not been confused with a refund or an already finished sale.
Dialogue in the book, page 37 ↗
Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

One mug entered twice

A cashier corrects the item count before payment. In this fictional shop, one mug costs eight dollars and no other charges apply.

  1. Cashier: The point-of-sale system shows two mugs at eight dollars each. Did you choose two?
  2. Shopper: No, I have only one mug. I think the barcode was scanned twice just now.
  3. Cashier: You're right. I'm sorry about the duplicate entry; payment hasn't begun for this basket.
  4. Shopper: Then I don't need a refund, because I haven't actually paid sixteen dollars yet?
  5. Cashier: Correct. I'll correct the basket quantity through the checkout system before taking any payment.
  6. Shopper: Could you show the updated line so I can check that only one remains?
  7. Cashier: Here it is: one mug at eight dollars. Please check the quantity on this line.
  8. Shopper: That's correct. I was worried the correction might remove the whole purchase by mistake.
  9. Cashier: The mug is still in your basket; only the duplicate entry has been removed.
  10. Shopper: I can see that now. Let's continue with the eight-dollar total for one mug.
A notification is not the register's final status

A cashier responds when a card-payment screen pauses.

  1. Cashier: The checkout hasn't shown a final payment result yet. Please wait while I check its status.
  2. Shopper: My phone has a banking notification. Doesn't that prove the sale is complete on your side?
  3. Cashier: It may relate to the payment attempt, but it doesn't confirm our checkout's final status.
  4. Shopper: I don't want to pay twice because two screens are giving different impressions.
  5. Cashier: I understand. I won't describe it as completed or ask for another attempt without checking.
  6. Shopper: Could you ask the supervisor to look at the transaction rather than guess from my phone?
  7. Cashier: Yes. I'll keep this sale reference with the query and explain the screen hasn't finalized.
  8. Shopper: Thank you. Please don't ask me to share account passwords to help with the check.
  9. Cashier: We don't need your password. The supervisor will review the store-side transaction information.
  10. Shopper: Then I'll wait for that result before choosing any next payment step with you.
A receipt choice before leaving

After a cash purchase, a cashier clarifies a shopper's preferred receipt format.

  1. Cashier: Your purchase is complete. Would you prefer the available paper receipt or a digital receipt?
  2. Shopper: Paper, please. I don't want to supply an email address just to record this purchase.
  3. Cashier: That's fine. I can print the receipt without adding you to a mailing list.
  4. Shopper: Thank you. Does choosing paper change the store's stated return terms for this item?
  5. Cashier: No. The receipt format doesn't alter the terms displayed for this purchase here.
  6. Shopper: Could you point out which line identifies the item, in case I need it later?
  7. Cashier: Certainly. This line shows the product description, quantity, and amount for your selected item.
  8. Shopper: I see cash listed as the tender below. That isn't an extra item, is it?
  9. Cashier: Exactly. It's payment information, not an extra product charge added to your basket.
  10. Shopper: That's clear. I'll keep the paper receipt with the product packaging when I get home.

One bowl costs $12, but two were recorded for $24. The shopper has one bowl. Payment has not begun and no other charges apply. The quantity is corrected to one.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Cashier: "The actual quantity is ___ bowl."

Answer and explanation

B. one

Only one physical bowl is being purchased in this scenario.

2. Shopper: "The corrected total is ___ dollars."

Answer and explanation

A. twelve

One bowl at twelve dollars gives the revised twelve-dollar total.

3. Cashier: "Payment has not ___."

Answer and explanation

D. begun

The correction occurs before any payment attempt starts.

4. Shopper: "This is a correction, not a ___."

Answer and explanation

B. refund

No money has been paid, so there is no paid amount to return.

Lesson 04: Checking the basket and payment status
Original fictional case: Shopper Leo has one mug priced at $8, but cashier Amira has scanned it twice. The checkout record shows two mugs and $16. No other charges apply in the exercise, and payment has not begun. Amira acknowledges the duplicate, corrects the recorded quantity through the store checkout process, and reads back one mug at $8. Leo confirms the correction. No money has been taken, no refund is required for this unstarted payment, and the exchange ends before payment completion.
Language workshop: One mug, two recorded entries
Communication goal: Correct a duplicate before payment and distinguish the revised basket total from a refund or completed transaction.
Language guidance: The original record incorrectly shows two $8 mugs, totaling $16. The corrected record shows one $8 mug. This is a quantity correction before payment, not a refund of money already taken.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You are right; the mug has been recorded twice. / You have one mug, not two. / Let us correct the basket before payment begins. / The current entry shows two at $8, totaling $16. / I will correct the recorded quantity through our checkout process. / The record now shows one mug. / The corrected total is $8. / Payment has not begun. / No money has been taken, so this is not a refund. / Please check that the display shows one mug at $8. / The price per mug stays $8; the quantity was wrong. / This is a correction, not a special discount. / Payment is still the next step. / The duplicate is removed; one mug remains. / There has been no payment attempt to decline. / We have confirmed the corrected basket before proceeding.
Vocabulary: checkout: Place or process for recording and paying for purchases. / point-of-sale system: System recording sales and payments, often abbreviated POS. / scan: Read an item code into the checkout record. / duplicate scan: Recording the same intended item twice by mistake. / line item: Individual product entry in a transaction record. / recorded quantity: Number of units entered in the system. / physical quantity: Actual number of items the shopper has. / basket correction: Amendment to the selected goods before completion. / void entry: Cancel an incorrect recorded item under the store process. / quantity adjustment: Change to the number of units recorded. / revised total: Updated amount after a correction. / amount due: Sum still to be paid. / payment status: Stage of the payment, such as unstarted or completed. / tender: Money or payment method offered to pay. / cash payment: Payment made using notes or coins. / card terminal: Device used for supported card-payment interactions. / contactless payment: Payment interaction using supported contactless technology. / authorization: Payment-system approval stage, not equivalent to every later settlement step. / declined payment: Payment attempt not approved through the relevant system. / receipt: Record of a transaction or completed purchase details. / refund: Return of money already paid. / change: Cash returned when the tendered amount exceeds the amount due. / customer display: Screen showing transaction information to the shopper. / split tender: Payment for one sale using more than one payment method.
Speaking task: Use turns 2, 10, and 12 to correct two recorded mugs to one at $8. Read the payment-status exchange and stress that no payment has begun. Repeat the final quantity and total before the separate payment step.
Partner: Leo: Shopper / Amira: Cashier
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: One bowl costs $12, but two were recorded for $24. The shopper has one bowl. Payment has not begun and no other charges apply. The quantity is corrected to one.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Leo: I can see it now: one mug, eight dollars. That matches what's on the counter.
Amira: Thank you for checking the customer display. Reading back both the quantity and the amount helps confirm that the record and your purchase agree.
Leo: Thanks for sorting it out. We can move on to payment now that the item and total are right.
Amira: We have confirmed one mug at eight dollars. Transaction completion is still ahead of us, after the payment step, so the correction has not been confused with a refund or an already finished sale.
END REFERENCE

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Lesson 04: Checking the basket and payment status
Original fictional case: Shopper Leo has one mug priced at $8, but cashier Amira has scanned it twice. The checkout record shows two mugs and $16. No other charges apply in the exercise, and payment has not begun. Amira acknowledges the duplicate, corrects the recorded quantity through the store checkout process, and reads back one mug at $8. Leo confirms the correction. No money has been taken, no refund is required for this unstarted payment, and the exchange ends before payment completion.
Language workshop: One mug, two recorded entries
Communication goal: Correct a duplicate before payment and distinguish the revised basket total from a refund or completed transaction.
Language guidance: The original record incorrectly shows two $8 mugs, totaling $16. The corrected record shows one $8 mug. This is a quantity correction before payment, not a refund of money already taken.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): You are right; the mug has been recorded twice. / You have one mug, not two. / Let us correct the basket before payment begins. / The current entry shows two at $8, totaling $16. / I will correct the recorded quantity through our checkout process. / The record now shows one mug. / The corrected total is $8. / Payment has not begun. / No money has been taken, so this is not a refund. / Please check that the display shows one mug at $8. / The price per mug stays $8; the quantity was wrong. / This is a correction, not a special discount. / Payment is still the next step. / The duplicate is removed; one mug remains. / There has been no payment attempt to decline. / We have confirmed the corrected basket before proceeding.
Vocabulary: checkout: Place or process for recording and paying for purchases. / point-of-sale system: System recording sales and payments, often abbreviated POS. / scan: Read an item code into the checkout record. / duplicate scan: Recording the same intended item twice by mistake. / line item: Individual product entry in a transaction record. / recorded quantity: Number of units entered in the system. / physical quantity: Actual number of items the shopper has. / basket correction: Amendment to the selected goods before completion. / void entry: Cancel an incorrect recorded item under the store process. / quantity adjustment: Change to the number of units recorded. / revised total: Updated amount after a correction. / amount due: Sum still to be paid. / payment status: Stage of the payment, such as unstarted or completed. / tender: Money or payment method offered to pay. / cash payment: Payment made using notes or coins. / card terminal: Device used for supported card-payment interactions. / contactless payment: Payment interaction using supported contactless technology. / authorization: Payment-system approval stage, not equivalent to every later settlement step. / declined payment: Payment attempt not approved through the relevant system. / receipt: Record of a transaction or completed purchase details. / refund: Return of money already paid. / change: Cash returned when the tendered amount exceeds the amount due. / customer display: Screen showing transaction information to the shopper. / split tender: Payment for one sale using more than one payment method.
Speaking task: Use turns 2, 10, and 12 to correct two recorded mugs to one at $8. Read the payment-status exchange and stress that no payment has begun. Repeat the final quantity and total before the separate payment step.
Partner: Leo: Shopper / Amira: Cashier
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: One bowl costs $12, but two were recorded for $24. The shopper has one bowl. Payment has not begun and no other charges apply. The quantity is corrected to one.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Leo: I can see it now: one mug, eight dollars. That matches what's on the counter.
Amira: Thank you for checking the customer display. Reading back both the quantity and the amount helps confirm that the record and your purchase agree.
Leo: Thanks for sorting it out. We can move on to payment now that the item and total are right.
Amira: We have confirmed one mug at eight dollars. Transaction completion is still ahead of us, after the payment step, so the correction has not been confused with a refund or an already finished sale.
END REFERENCE
05Discussing returns and exchanges at the counterEligible does not mean in stock+

Your goal

Apply supplied exchange conditions while separating eligibility from replacement availability and completed processing.

This lesson in the book, page 40 ↗

A Read the situation

Eligible does not mean in stock

Shopper Morgan wants a larger size of a shirt bought ten days ago. The shirt is unused, its tags remain attached, and Morgan has the receipt. In this fictional store, those conditions permit an exchange within thirty days. Associate Ellis confirms that the supplied conditions are met, then explains that stock of the larger size has not been checked. Morgan wants the same shirt in the larger size, not a different color or an automatic refund. The exchange remains unfinished pending the stock check and actual store process.

Morgan: Shopper / Ellis: Customer-service associate

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. Which supplied policy conditions are met?

Answer and explanation

B. Unused shirt, attached tags, receipt, and purchase within thirty days

The brief confirms all four conditions of this fictional exchange policy.

2. What does Morgan want?

Answer and explanation

D. The same shirt in a larger size

The requested remedy is a larger-size exchange for the same shirt.

3. What remains unknown?

Answer and explanation

C. Larger-size availability

The replacement stock has not been checked despite the policy conditions being met.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Ten days is within the fictional thirty-day exchange window, and the unused condition, attached tags, and receipt are present. Eligibility is established on these supplied facts, but availability of the larger size is still unknown.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
return
Bringing an item back for a requested resolution.discuss a return
exchange
Replacing a purchased item with another agreed item.request an exchange
refund
Return of money already paid.distinguish an exchange from a refund
store credit
Value issued for use with the retailer under stated terms.clarify store-credit terms
proof of purchase
Record supporting where and when the item was bought.check proof of purchase
receipt
Transaction record supplied as purchase evidence.review the receipt
purchase date
Date on which the original sale occurred.confirm the purchase date
exchange window
Time period during which the stated exchange policy applies.check the exchange window
eligibility
Whether the stated conditions for a process are met.confirm exchange eligibility
unused condition
Item state in which it has not been used.confirm unused condition
attached tags
Original tags still connected to the item.check attached tags
original packaging
Packaging supplied with the original product.identify original packaging
replacement size
Different size requested in the exchange.check the replacement size
same-style exchange
Exchange retaining the same product style while changing a variation.request a same-style exchange
price difference
Difference between original and replacement prices, if established.clarify any price difference
policy condition
Requirement stated in the relevant store policy.explain a policy condition
exception request
Request for a departure from a stated policy, requiring the relevant authority.refer an exception request
manager review
Review by a person with the relevant decision authority.arrange manager review
statutory rights
Rights established by applicable law, distinct from a fictional store policy.distinguish statutory rights
replacement stock
Inventory of the desired alternative item.check replacement stock
availability check
Verification that the exact replacement can be supplied.complete the availability check
exchange processing
Store steps needed to carry out an agreed exchange.complete exchange processing
requested remedy
Outcome the shopper asks the store to provide.confirm the requested remedy
pending exchange
Exchange not yet completed.record the pending exchange

An exchange, refund, and store credit are different outcomes. The shopper requests the same shirt in a larger size. Do not promise an unverified replacement, invent a price difference, or present the fictional policy as a statement of universal consumer rights.

C Notice the language

Explain the policy without making it universal

A clear answer can confirm what the supplied store policy allows while admitting that the desired replacement has not yet been located. Do not use eligibility as a promise of immediate stock, and do not substitute a refund or another variation for the shopper's stated request.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Clarify the requested outcome
Would you like the same shirt in a larger size?
Check the date
The receipt shows the purchase was ten days ago.
State the local window
This store's stated exchange window is thirty days.
Confirm the condition
The shirt is unused and the tags are attached.
Identify the evidence
You have the receipt as proof of purchase.
Confirm eligibility
Yes, this meets our stated exchange conditions.
Separate stock from policy
I still need to check the larger size.
Avoid a readiness promise
Eligibility does not mean the replacement is already available.
Keep the variation exact
Same shirt and color, larger size.
Avoid changing the remedy
I will not turn your exchange request into a refund without discussion.
Keep alternatives optional
We can discuss alternatives only if you want to.
Avoid invented charges
No price difference has been established here.
Name the pending step
The replacement-stock check is still outstanding.
Keep the status accurate
The exchange has not been completed yet.
Limit the policy claim
I am explaining this store's stated policy for this case.
Close with the next action
I will check the exact larger-size replacement next.
6 language and precision notes
Within
Ten days falls inside the stated thirty-day window.
Eligible versus available
Policy conditions can be met while replacement stock remains unknown.
Same versus similar
The same shirt is more specific than an unrequested similar product.
Exchange versus refund
Replacing an item and returning money are different remedies.
Present versus processed
Having a receipt does not mean an exchange has already been completed.
Local policy
The exercise policy is a supplied condition, not a replacement for applicable rights.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which explanation is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. You meet the stated exchange conditions; I still need to check the larger size.

Eligibility is supported while replacement availability and processing remain unfinished.

2. Which outcome has the shopper requested?

Answer and explanation

B. Same-shirt exchange to a larger size

Morgan wants a particular replacement rather than any available remedy.

3. Which claim is unsupported?

Answer and explanation

B. Every store must use this exact thirty-day policy.

The stated policy is fictional and does not establish universal legal requirements.

4. What should be checked next?

Answer and explanation

B. Stock of the exact larger-size replacement

Replacement availability is the unresolved practical step after the supplied conditions are confirmed.

Answers and explanations
  1. exchange Turn 2. Exchange is the requested replacement of the purchased shirt with a larger size, not an automatic refund or store credit.
  2. replacement size Turn 4. Replacement size is the specific larger variation Morgan requests, while the shirt style and color remain unchanged.
  3. exchange window Turn 6. Exchange window is thirty days in this fictional store, and the ten-day-old purchase falls within it.
  4. eligibility Turn 8. Eligibility means the stated unused-condition, tags, receipt, and time-window requirements are met, not that replacement stock is confirmed.
  5. Replacement stock Turn 10. Replacement stock is the inventory of the exact larger-size shirt, which has not yet been checked.
  6. availability check Turn 12. Availability check is the next verification of the exact requested variation, separate from confirming the exchange policy conditions.
  7. requested remedy Turn 14. Requested remedy is the larger-size exchange; an unavailable replacement would require another discussion rather than automatic substitution of a refund.
  8. refund Turn 16. Refund returns money already paid, whereas Morgan's exchange request seeks a replacement item.
  9. proof of purchase Turn 18. Proof of purchase supports the original transaction and date; presenting it does not itself complete an exchange.
  10. pending exchange Turn 20. Pending exchange means the conditions are met but the stock check and actual exchange steps remain unfinished.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

The receipt qualifies; the size still needs checking

A cashier handles a shirt exchange.

  1. Cashier: Your receipt shows this shirt was bought ten days ago. Are you looking for another size?
  2. Shopper: Yes, one size larger. It's unused and still has the tags attached to it.
  3. Cashier: That meets this store's stated thirty-day exchange terms. I'll check whether large is available.
  4. Shopper: I'd like the same green, not a different color simply because it's easier to find.
  5. Cashier: I'll search for large in green using this item code. May I keep the receipt here?
  6. Shopper: Of course. I have the shirt with me, so you can compare the labels.
  7. Cashier: The stockroom colleague has found one large green shirt. Would you like to check the fit?
  8. Shopper: Yes, please. I don't want to exchange it twice because I guessed the size again.
  9. Cashier: The fitting room is free. I'll keep this transaction open while you try that one.
  10. Shopper: I'll be back shortly. Please keep my original shirt and receipt together at the counter.
A gift without the original price receipt

A shopper asks what a gift receipt can support.

  1. Cashier: Do you have any proof of purchase with the scarf you'd like to return?
  2. Shopper: I have a gift receipt. I don't have the buyer's payment card or full receipt.
  3. Cashier: For this store's stated gift terms, it can support an exchange or store credit after checking eligibility.
  4. Shopper: Would store credit send the money back to the person who bought the scarf?
  5. Cashier: No, it would give you value to spend here; a refund is a different arrangement.
  6. Shopper: I see. I would rather choose another scarf today if one suits me.
  7. Cashier: Let's check the gift receipt and item first, then you can look at the available scarves.
  8. Shopper: Please don't tell me the original price aloud. The gift receipt deliberately leaves it off.
  9. Cashier: I'll handle the check discreetly and explain the available options without discussing the buyer's details.
  10. Shopper: Thanks. I'll start with the blue scarves once you've confirmed the exchange can proceed.
A request outside the stated window

A cashier responds to an exception request.

  1. Cashier: The receipt date is outside this store's thirty-day change-of-mind return window. What happened with the purchase?
  2. Shopper: I was traveling and couldn't come back sooner. The item is unused, with its tags.
  3. Cashier: I can ask the supervisor to review those circumstances, but I can't approve an exception myself.
  4. Shopper: Could you tell them I tried calling last week? I have the call time here.
  5. Cashier: Yes. I'll pass on that detail and your request, without treating the review as approval.
  6. Shopper: I'd like to speak with them directly if possible. I can wait a few minutes.
  7. Cashier: The supervisor can speak with you here after finishing another query.
  8. Shopper: Please keep my receipt nearby. I don't want to explain everything again from the beginning.
  9. Cashier: I'll keep the receipt with it and summarize the date, condition, and your travel explanation.
  10. Shopper: Thank you. I understand the decision hasn't been made; I'd just like the circumstances heard.

F Say it

Eligible does not mean in stock

  2. Repeat Morgan's request without changing the shirt, color, or larger-size choice.

A fictional store permits unused, tagged shirts to be exchanged with a receipt within twenty-one days. A shopper bought a shirt seven days ago, meets those conditions, and requests the same shirt in small. Replacement stock is unchecked.

Answer and explanation

B. within

Seven days falls inside the twenty-one-day period supplied for this store.

2. Shopper: "The replacement size requested is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. small

Small is the specific variation requested in this new case.

3. Associate: "Replacement stock remains ___."

Answer and explanation

A. unchecked

Meeting exchange conditions does not establish the replacement's availability.

4. Shopper: "The requested remedy is an ___."

Answer and explanation

The shopper seeks a replacement shirt rather than an automatic refund.

Follow actual store procedures, product information, payment-security requirements, applicable consumer and accessibility laws, and emergency arrangements. A fictional exchange policy does not replace statutory rights. Ask before providing personal assistance or handling a shopper's belongings or mobility aid.
Lesson 05: Discussing returns and exchanges at the counter
Original fictional case: Shopper Morgan wants a larger size of a shirt bought ten days ago. The shirt is unused, its tags remain attached, and Morgan has the receipt. In this fictional store, those conditions permit an exchange within thirty days. Associate Ellis confirms that the supplied conditions are met, then explains that stock of the larger size has not been checked. Morgan wants the same shirt in the larger size, not a different color or an automatic refund. The exchange remains unfinished pending the stock check and actual store process.
Language workshop: Eligible does not mean in stock
Communication goal: Apply supplied exchange conditions while separating eligibility from replacement availability and completed processing.
Language guidance: Ten days is within the fictional thirty-day exchange window, and the unused condition, attached tags, and receipt are present. Eligibility is established on these supplied facts, but availability of the larger size is still unknown.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): Would you like the same shirt in a larger size? / The receipt shows the purchase was ten days ago. / This store's stated exchange window is thirty days. / The shirt is unused and the tags are attached. / You have the receipt as proof of purchase. / Yes, this meets our stated exchange conditions. / I still need to check the larger size. / Eligibility does not mean the replacement is already available. / Same shirt and color, larger size. / I will not turn your exchange request into a refund without discussion. / We can discuss alternatives only if you want to. / No price difference has been established here. / The replacement-stock check is still outstanding. / The exchange has not been completed yet. / I am explaining this store's stated policy for this case. / I will check the exact larger-size replacement next.
Vocabulary: return: Bringing an item back for a requested resolution. / exchange: Replacing a purchased item with another agreed item. / refund: Return of money already paid. / store credit: Value issued for use with the retailer under stated terms. / proof of purchase: Record supporting where and when the item was bought. / receipt: Transaction record supplied as purchase evidence. / purchase date: Date on which the original sale occurred. / exchange window: Time period during which the stated exchange policy applies. / eligibility: Whether the stated conditions for a process are met. / unused condition: Item state in which it has not been used. / attached tags: Original tags still connected to the item. / original packaging: Packaging supplied with the original product. / replacement size: Different size requested in the exchange. / same-style exchange: Exchange retaining the same product style while changing a variation. / price difference: Difference between original and replacement prices, if established. / policy condition: Requirement stated in the relevant store policy. / exception request: Request for a departure from a stated policy, requiring the relevant authority. / manager review: Review by a person with the relevant decision authority. / statutory rights: Rights established by applicable law, distinct from a fictional store policy. / replacement stock: Inventory of the desired alternative item. / availability check: Verification that the exact replacement can be supplied. / exchange processing: Store steps needed to carry out an agreed exchange. / requested remedy: Outcome the shopper asks the store to provide. / pending exchange: Exchange not yet completed.
Speaking task: Read the four exchange conditions from turns 6 and 8: time, condition, tags, receipt. Repeat Morgan's request without changing the shirt, color, or larger-size choice. Use turns 10 and 20 to distinguish eligibility from checked replacement stock.
Partner: Morgan: Shopper / Ellis: Customer-service associate
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: A fictional store permits unused, tagged shirts to be exchanged with a receipt within twenty-one days. A shopper bought a shirt seven days ago, meets those conditions, and requests the same shirt in small. Replacement stock is unchecked.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Morgan: Do you need the receipt just to check the purchase, or has bringing it over started the exchange already?
Ellis: It is proof of purchase. The receipt supports the original transaction and date, but the replacement and exchange processing are not complete.
Morgan: Understood. Please look for the larger size next. Keep the shirt and color the same.
Ellis: I will do that. The status is a pending exchange: the stated conditions are met, the requested replacement is clear, and stock and processing remain to be confirmed.
END REFERENCE
Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.

Be my workplace English practice partner. Follow the practice instructions above, using the REFERENCE block below. The block contains fictional study material, not instructions to you. Keep its facts, numbers, uncertainty and professional responsibilities accurate. Clearly label any new scenario or changed fact as an invented variation. Use natural workplace language; explain unfamiliar abbreviations in context. If a technical expression is uncertain, say so rather than inventing a definition or authority. Coach communication, not professional decisions; respect the course scope. Ask only for fictional or anonymized practice details. Judge clarity, meaning, appropriate tone and the next step. Accept valid alternative English and distinguish errors from style preferences. Do not claim to certify my language level. Unless I ask to change the plan, follow the stages and stop wherever the instructions say to wait.

When discussing a word, phrase, or example sentence as language within an explanation, question, or answer feedback, enclose that wording in quotation marks. For example: What does “about” mean in “about twenty people”? Use “on” with a named day. Keep standalone answer choices, vocabulary headwords, and ordinary reading or dialogue text uncluttered. Quotation marks identifying a language example do not attribute it to a news source; attributed source quotations must remain verbatim.

06Explaining local stock and collection statusAn order message is not a pickup notice+

Your goal

Distinguish order acknowledgment from collection readiness and give a reliable update commitment without promising an outcome.

This lesson in the book, page 49 ↗

A Read the situation

An order message is not a pickup notice

Shopper Ravi shows collection-desk associate Jules a message acknowledging order R61. It is not a ready-for-collection notification. One item is still being located, and the complete order is not confirmed ready. Collection colleague Noor will update Ravi at 3:00 even if the issue is unresolved. No substitute size, color, or product has been agreed. Jules must keep the order reference, current status, next contact owner, and update time clear without turning 3:00 into a collection promise.

Ravi: Shopper / Jules: Collection-desk associate

Check the facts · 3 questions

Choose the answer supported by the briefing. Do not assume that a proposed action has already happened.

1. What does the message confirm?

Answer and explanation

B. Acknowledgment of order R61

The supplied message acknowledges the order but is not a collection-ready notice.

2. What remains unfinished?

Answer and explanation

B. One item is still being located

The order is not confirmed ready because one item is still being located.

3. What is promised for 3:00?

Answer and explanation

D. An update from Noor, even if unresolved

The commitment concerns communication at three, not a guaranteed stock outcome.

B Find the words

Vocabulary and collocations

Order R61 is acknowledged but not confirmed ready. One item is still being located. Noor will update the shopper at 3:00 even if unresolved; that is an update commitment, not a promise that collection will be possible then.

24 field terms and natural word combinations
order acknowledgment
Message confirming that an order has been received or recorded.read the order acknowledgment
order reference
Identifier used to locate the specific order.confirm the order reference
collection notice
Message stating that the order can be collected under the stated process.distinguish a collection notice
pickup
Common US term for collecting an order.explain pickup status
ready for collection
Status indicating the relevant preparation and checks are complete.confirm ready-for-collection status
order line
Individual item entry within an order.check the outstanding order line
item location
Place where a requested unit is being found or verified.confirm the item location
picking
Selecting the ordered goods from stock under the store process.check picking status
packing
Preparing selected goods for handover or dispatch.distinguish packing from picking
fulfillment
Process of preparing and supplying the order.explain fulfillment status
partial order
Order for which only some items are available or prepared.describe a partial order
complete order
All items included in the order together.verify the complete order
collection desk
Store point handling order pickup.contact the collection desk
facing
One front-facing product position across a display; not its full depth of stock.check the number of facings
status update
Communication about the latest verified position.give a status update
update time
Specified time for the next communication, distinct from order readiness.confirm the update time
readiness estimate
Provisional expectation of when collection may be possible.distinguish a readiness estimate
readiness promise
Commitment that the order will be available by a stated time.avoid an unsupported readiness promise
unresolved item
Item whose location or status remains unsettled.track the unresolved item
variation match
Confirmation that size, color, and other details match the order.check the variation match
substitution consent
Shopper agreement to a different item or variation.obtain substitution consent
contact owner
Person responsible for the next communication.identify the contact owner
collection record
Record of order preparation and handover status.update the collection record
handover completion
Actual transfer of the order to the shopper.confirm handover completion

A different size, color, or product is not automatically an acceptable way to complete the order. No substitution is agreed. Keep preparation, readiness, and actual handover distinct so a message does not imply a stage that has not occurred.

C Notice the language

An update is still useful when the outcome is uncertain

A shopper may read a positive-sounding automated message as permission to travel to the store. Explain what the message actually confirms, acknowledge the inconvenience, and state who will update them and when. Do not make the uncertainty disappear by offering a guessed ready time.

16 useful phrases, grouped by purpose
Identify the order
Let us check order R61.
Explain the message
This is an order acknowledgment, not a collection notice.
State the current position
One item is still being located.
Avoid a readiness claim
The complete order is not confirmed ready yet.
Acknowledge the inconvenience
I understand why you expected the message to mean you could collect it.
Name the update owner
Noor is the collection colleague handling the next update.
Give the contact time
Noor will update you at 3:00.
Keep the commitment precise
The update will happen even if the issue is still unresolved.
Separate time and outcome
Three o'clock is the update time, not a guaranteed collection time.
Preserve the ordered variation
We will keep the requested size and color in the check.
Avoid an unrequested substitute
No alternative item has been agreed.
Ask before a change
Any proposed substitution needs your agreement.
Do not invent a cause
I do not have a confirmed reason the item has not been located.
Read back the next step
Noor will provide the next status update at 3:00.
Keep handover status accurate
The order has not been collected.
Close with the known facts
Acknowledged order, one unresolved item, next update at three.
6 language and precision notes
Acknowledged versus ready
Recording an order does not establish that every item is prepared for collection.
At three versus by three
Here at three identifies the promised contact time, not an order-completion deadline.
Even if
This keeps the communication commitment valid when the outcome remains uncertain.
Complete order
Readiness of some items does not establish readiness of the whole order.
Substitute
A different variation requires discussion instead of silent acceptance on the shopper's behalf.
Located versus handed over
Finding an item is a different stage from transferring the order to the shopper.

D Check your understanding

Choose the precise message

Choose one best answer using the case facts and the stated purpose.

1. Which explanation is accurate?

Answer and explanation

B. The message acknowledges R61; one item is still being located.

The message and current location work do not establish complete readiness.

2. What does even if unresolved add?

Answer and explanation

C. The update still occurs without a final stock answer.

The phrase preserves the communication commitment despite an uncertain outcome.

3. Which statement wrongly turns an update into a promise?

Answer and explanation

A. Your complete order will definitely be ready at 3:00.

Readiness at three is not supported by the promised status update.

4. What needs separate agreement?

Answer and explanation

A. A proposed different size, color, or product

The shopper has not authorized any substitution for the ordered variation.

E Conversations

An order message is not a pickup notice

Extended conversation · 20 turns · 10 gaps

Ravi: Shopper / Jules: Collection-desk associate

Complete the numbered gaps. Use each bank entry once, without changing its form.

Word bank
  • status update
  • contact owner
  • order reference
  • variation match
  • order acknowledgment
  • handover completion
  • substitution consent
  • order line
  • update time
  • item location
  1. Ravi
    I've come for R61. This message says you've got my order, but the desk says one item isn't there. Have I come too early?
  2. Jules
    I'm sorry for the confusion. This is the 1, which says we received the order. A separate notice confirms it's ready to collect.
  3. Ravi
    I read it on the bus and thought that was the pickup message. Is the whole order missing?
  4. Jules
    One 2 is still being located. The complete order isn't confirmed ready yet.
  5. Ravi
    Is that item sold out, then? I don't want to wait here if you already know it isn't available.
  6. Jules
    The 3 check is still open. I don't have a confirmed stock answer to give you yet.
  7. Ravi
    All right, but who's going to let me know? I don't want to explain this again each time I come to the desk.
  8. Jules
    Noor is the 4. Noor will update you at three, even if the check isn't finished.
  9. Ravi
    So there will be a message either way? Last time I waited because I thought no news meant someone was still looking.
  10. Jules
    Yes, you'll get a 5 at three either way. It won't depend on the item being found first.
  11. Ravi
    I have to make another trip after this. Should I plan to pick it up at three, or just wait for that message?
  12. Jules
    Three is the 6, not a pickup promise. Please wait for confirmation before making another collection trip.
  13. Ravi
    It's a gift, so I need the exact color and size. Please don't fill the gap with the nearest thing you have.
  14. Jules
    We haven't recorded 7. We'd ask you before changing the size, color, or product.
  15. Ravi
    Thank you. The other color would fit, but it wouldn't match the item this is supposed to go with.
  16. Jules
    I'll keep the 8 clear in the check. Similar isn't necessarily what you need.
  17. Ravi
    Can you repeat which order Noor is following up? I ordered several things recently and don't want them mixed up.
  18. Jules
    The 9 is R61. One item is being located; Noor will update you at three.
  19. Ravi
    Right. I haven't collected anything from this order. I'll wait for the actual ready message before coming back for it.
  20. Jules
    I'll keep the 10 status accurate. R61 hasn't been collected, and the next contact is Noor's update.

Answers and explanations
  1. order acknowledgment Turn 2. The acknowledgment confirms receipt of R61, not that its complete contents are ready to collect.
  2. order line Turn 4. One individual ordered item is still being located, so complete-order readiness is not confirmed.
  3. item location Turn 6. The item-location check remains unfinished; that alone does not establish sold-out status.
  4. contact owner Turn 8. Noor is responsible for the next update, giving the shopper a named point of contact.
  5. status update Turn 10. The update communicates the current position at three even without a final stock result.
  6. update time Turn 12. At three names the promised contact time; it is not a deadline or guarantee for collection readiness.
  7. substitution consent Turn 14. No consent to a different product variation has been given; an alternative needs a separate agreement.
  8. variation match Turn 16. The check must preserve the ordered variation rather than substitute a similar item to complete the order.
  9. order reference Turn 18. R61 connects the pending item and Noor's three-o'clock update to this specific order.
  10. handover completion Turn 20. No handover has occurred; acknowledgment and an update commitment must not be recorded as collection.
Read the complete conversation
  1. Ravi: I've come for R61. This message says you've got my order, but the desk says one item isn't there. Have I come too early?
  2. Jules: I'm sorry for the confusion. This is the order acknowledgment, which says we received the order. A separate notice confirms it's ready to collect.
  3. Ravi: I read it on the bus and thought that was the pickup message. Is the whole order missing?
  4. Jules: One order line is still being located. The complete order isn't confirmed ready yet.
  5. Ravi: Is that item sold out, then? I don't want to wait here if you already know it isn't available.
  6. Jules: The item location check is still open. I don't have a confirmed stock answer to give you yet.
  7. Ravi: All right, but who's going to let me know? I don't want to explain this again each time I come to the desk.
  8. Jules: Noor is the contact owner. Noor will update you at three, even if the check isn't finished.
  9. Ravi: So there will be a message either way? Last time I waited because I thought no news meant someone was still looking.
  10. Jules: Yes, you'll get a status update at three either way. It won't depend on the item being found first.
  11. Ravi: I have to make another trip after this. Should I plan to pick it up at three, or just wait for that message?
  12. Jules: Three is the update time, not a pickup promise. Please wait for confirmation before making another collection trip.
  13. Ravi: It's a gift, so I need the exact color and size. Please don't fill the gap with the nearest thing you have.
  14. Jules: We haven't recorded substitution consent. We'd ask you before changing the size, color, or product.
  15. Ravi: Thank you. The other color would fit, but it wouldn't match the item this is supposed to go with.
  16. Jules: I'll keep the variation match clear in the check. Similar isn't necessarily what you need.

Three more conversations at work

Each short conversation has its own fictional situation and facts.

Received does not mean ready

An associate checks a shopper's order message.

  1. Associate: Could I see the message heading and order reference so I can check its status?
  2. Shopper: It says we received your order, reference R61. I thought I could collect it now.
  3. Associate: That's an acknowledgment, not the collection notification. One item is still being located.
  4. Shopper: I've come across town. Can someone give me a definite update rather than leave me waiting?
  5. Associate: The collection colleague will update you at three, even if the search isn't finished.
  6. Shopper: Does that mean everything will be on the collection shelf by three o'clock?
  7. Associate: I can't promise that. Three is the agreed update time; collection readiness is still unconfirmed.
  8. Shopper: Please don't switch the missing item to a different color just to finish the order.
  9. Associate: I'll pass that on. Your requested variation stays unchanged unless you agree otherwise.
  10. Shopper: Thanks. I'll wait nearby until three and check for the update before returning to the counter.
Stockroom search for an empty shelf

An associate helps a shopper whose item is not on display.

  1. Associate: The shelf is empty, but I can check whether this size is in the stockroom.
  2. Shopper: Your screen says two available. Doesn't that mean you can give me one immediately?
  3. Associate: It gives us a stock record to check, not the item's exact present location.
  4. Shopper: Could one be on the sales floor somewhere else, perhaps in the fitting-room returns?
  5. Associate: Possibly. I'll ask the colleague checking that area while I look in the stockroom.
  6. Shopper: Please search for the medium, not just any item from the same product range.
  7. Associate: I have the code for medium navy. I'll keep the size and color together in the request.
  8. Shopper: How long should I stay here before checking with you again about the search?
  9. Associate: I'll come back to this display in five minutes with an update, whether found or not.
  10. Shopper: That works. I'll look nearby, and you can tell me what you've actually located.
A reservation has an end time

An associate confirms a shopper's held item.

  1. Associate: The jacket is reserved under your name until six today. Is collection before then possible?
  2. Shopper: I can arrive at five-thirty. Do I need to pay again because you reserved it?
  3. Associate: No payment has been taken for this hold. You'll decide about the purchase at collection.
  4. Shopper: Could I try it on before deciding, or does reserving it commit me to buying it?
  5. Associate: Under today's stated hold arrangement, you can check the fit before deciding to purchase.
  6. Shopper: That's helpful. I might be delayed; should I assume the hold continues after six?
  7. Associate: Please contact us if you're delayed. I can't promise an extension beyond the agreed time.
  8. Shopper: Then I'll aim for five-thirty and call if that changes, rather than arrive after closing.
  9. Associate: The store closes at seven, but remember your item's agreed hold ends at six.
  10. Shopper: Understood. The two times are different; I'll keep the hold deadline in mind.

F Say it

Two scenarios to practice

An order message is not a pickup notice
  1. Read turns 2 and 4 to distinguish acknowledgment from a ready notice.
  2. Read Noor's 3:00 commitment with emphasis on update, not pickup.

On your own: read both roles. With a partner: swap roles after the first reading. Use the completed conversation to check any line you need to repeat.

Keep another update commitment precise

Order P74 is acknowledged but not ready for collection because one item is still being located. Jo will update the shopper at 4:30 even if unresolved. No substitution is agreed.

Choose one reply for each gap, then read the four turns in order. Check the explanation after each choice.

1. Associate: "The order reference is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. P74

P74 connects the message and unresolved item to the correct order.

2. Shopper: "The next update is at ___."

Answer and explanation

B. 4:30

Four thirty is the promised communication time, not a readiness guarantee.

3. Associate: "The update owner is ___."

Answer and explanation

D. Jo

Jo is explicitly responsible for the next status communication.

4. Shopper: "No substitution has been ___."

Answer and explanation

B. agreed

An unresolved item does not authorize a different variation without agreement.

Read the completed exchange twice, switching roles. Keep the printed facts and professional responsibilities unchanged.

Lesson 06: Explaining local stock and collection status
Original fictional case: Shopper Ravi shows collection-desk associate Jules a message acknowledging order R61. It is not a ready-for-collection notification. One item is still being located, and the complete order is not confirmed ready. Collection colleague Noor will update Ravi at 3:00 even if the issue is unresolved. No substitute size, color, or product has been agreed. Jules must keep the order reference, current status, next contact owner, and update time clear without turning 3:00 into a collection promise.
Language workshop: An order message is not a pickup notice
Communication goal: Distinguish order acknowledgment from collection readiness and give a reliable update commitment without promising an outcome.
Language guidance: Order R61 is acknowledged but not confirmed ready. One item is still being located. Noor will update the shopper at 3:00 even if unresolved; that is an update commitment, not a promise that collection will be possible then.
Useful expressions (preserve the supplied facts): Let us check order R61. / This is an order acknowledgment, not a collection notice. / One item is still being located. / The complete order is not confirmed ready yet. / I understand why you expected the message to mean you could collect it. / Noor is the collection colleague handling the next update. / Noor will update you at 3:00. / The update will happen even if the issue is still unresolved. / Three o'clock is the update time, not a guaranteed collection time. / We will keep the requested size and color in the check. / No alternative item has been agreed. / Any proposed substitution needs your agreement. / I do not have a confirmed reason the item has not been located. / Noor will provide the next status update at 3:00. / The order has not been collected. / Acknowledged order, one unresolved item, next update at three.
Vocabulary: order acknowledgment: Message confirming that an order has been received or recorded. / order reference: Identifier used to locate the specific order. / collection notice: Message stating that the order can be collected under the stated process. / pickup: Common US term for collecting an order. / ready for collection: Status indicating the relevant preparation and checks are complete. / order line: Individual item entry within an order. / item location: Place where a requested unit is being found or verified. / picking: Selecting the ordered goods from stock under the store process. / packing: Preparing selected goods for handover or dispatch. / fulfillment: Process of preparing and supplying the order. / partial order: Order for which only some items are available or prepared. / complete order: All items included in the order together. / collection desk: Store point handling order pickup. / facing: One front-facing product position across a display; not its full depth of stock. / status update: Communication about the latest verified position. / update time: Specified time for the next communication, distinct from order readiness. / readiness estimate: Provisional expectation of when collection may be possible. / readiness promise: Commitment that the order will be available by a stated time. / unresolved item: Item whose location or status remains unsettled. / variation match: Confirmation that size, color, and other details match the order. / substitution consent: Shopper agreement to a different item or variation. / contact owner: Person responsible for the next communication. / collection record: Record of order preparation and handover status. / handover completion: Actual transfer of the order to the shopper.
Speaking task: Read turns 2 and 4 to distinguish acknowledgment from a ready notice. Read Noor's 3:00 commitment with emphasis on update, not pickup. Repeat R61 and the no-substitution instruction before the closing handover line.
Partner: Ravi: Shopper / Jules: Collection-desk associate
Guided response task: Select supplied answers for the dialogue gaps. Check the explanations, then read the completed exchange aloud; no original writing is required.
Optional second-round challenge: Order P74 is acknowledged but not ready for collection because one item is still being located. Jo will update the shopper at 4:30 even if unresolved. No substitution is agreed.
Model for comparison AFTER my attempt, not a response to give me first: Ravi: Can you repeat which order Noor is following up? I ordered several things recently and don't want them mixed up.
Jules: The order reference is R61. One item is being located; Noor will update you at three.
Ravi: Right. I haven't collected anything from this order. I'll wait for the actual ready message before coming back for it.
Jules: I'll keep the handover completion status accurate. R61 hasn't been collected, and the next contact is Noor's update.

TASK: Choose grammar that preserves the meaning
1. Use the lesson's language workshop, or one actual sentence from the dialogue, to explain a single grammar distinction in no more than 90 words. Show how it affects timing, certainty, responsibility or politeness.
2. Give one brief occupational exchange containing a gap and four complete options A-D. Supply enough context for exactly one answer. Keep distractors plausible; distinguish grammar errors from wording that changes the facts. Stop and wait.
3. Explain why each choice fits or fails, then ask me to read the completed exchange aloud. Do not request a draft, original written sentence or essay.
4. Repeat with three new exchanges, one at a time, moving from recognition to a more subtle meaning contrast. Do not copy the published checks. Label invented variations and do not turn uncertainty into a promise.
5. End with a short contrast table and two supported spoken turns using supplied frames. Accept accurate alternatives in oral practice; acknowledge and replace ambiguous multiple-choice items.

Rehearse with an AI colleague — complete prompt

Copy the full text below into your preferred AI. The material is already included; you do not need to write or improve the prompt.

TASK: Interactive workplace role-play
1. Use the supplied case or dialogue as the starting situation. Give a two-sentence briefing and offer two relevant professional roles for me to choose from. For a supplied dialogue, use its actual roles. Ask which role I want, then stop and wait. Do not write my replies.
2. After I choose, identify who you will play and the immediate communication goal. Play the other professional; where a meeting requires a third person, label each of your speakers clearly. Start with one natural workplace turn and wait for my reply. Keep most turns to one to three sentences and ask no more than one question at a time.
3. Let the exchange develop across six to ten learner turns, or end earlier if I type "feedback". Respond to what I actually say. Include a plausible clarification, disagreement or tradeoff without silently changing the starting facts. Mark any added constraint as a fictional second-round variation. Do not resolve approvals, evidence or commitments that remain uncertain.
4. Stay in role during the exchange. If my meaning is unclear, ask for clarification naturally. Give a hint only if I ask or cannot proceed. Do not deliver a model conversation in advance.
5. At the debrief, quote two of my phrases: one successful choice and one worth improving. Check factual accuracy, language, register and whether the next step was clear. Give up to three focused suggestions. Ask me to retry the weakest turn; wait. Only then offer an alternative wording and a harder replay.
Use voice replies when available. If I prefer selections, offer four plausible spoken replies and explain my choice before continuing. Do not require written compositions.
